The purpose of animal rights is not to ensure that humans and animals are equal, but to ensure the animals' basic rights and freedoms, to suffer and torture, to starve, to be torn from their natural habitats and brutally murdered for simple pleasures. In 2004, we demonstrated our sensitivity to animal protection and preventing violence against animals through Act 5199, which we established in the first term of our AK PARTY rule as a separate law. Now we've prepared our proposal, considering the growing expectations of our citizens and the needs of our dear friends in the face of new events that have arisen over the past 17 years. We aim to provide both human and animal well-being, health and safety by offering a law that eases the conscience of our citizens and society. In light of the measures and recommendations set by the report, I see our proposal of law as a historic step in both preparation and the way it deals with this delicate issue. The most important amendment to this bill is that animals are reduced from property and property and evaluated in living status. All household pets, which are considered without commercial purpose, are banned from their owners due to their debts, and the terms concerning animals are removed from the law. No cats or dogs are allowed in the pet shop. Also -- a very important point -- in the current law as a misdemeanor -- is to kill animals that are in danger of extinction, destroy an animal generation, kill a pet or pet on purpose, torture it, brutalize it, fight animals, rape animals, possession or unclaimed, and increase the amount of administrative fines available in terms of the other acts of misdemeanor. I'd like to explain to you a few examples of the profound difference between guilt and crime. When these verbs become crimes, prison sentences can be served, and punishments will achieve their goal in deterrentism. There is no such thing as a criminal record, which will be entered into the criminal record of those who commit these verbs. In terms of the verbs organised as a crime with this new arrangement, prosecutors and judges will step in immediately. In its former way, there was no such opportunity under the blame. Even these details alone are important to illustrate the difference between crime and guilt. I believe it will bring with it a mental transformation. In this dimension, our proposal for the law can be viewed as the beginning of a new era and as an age. Mr. President, my esteemed fellow lawmakers, I believe that we've been working overtime to ensure that animals are more effectively protected, and I believe that our proposal is enough to please all sides of the matter.
